ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u2	Mr President, you will allow me to speak from my point of view, because it is an issue which, as you have said quite rightly, has almost gone through unanimously, with the exception of one reservation. As a result, and once this consensus and unanimity are in place, we simply need to make some points on the subject, which we shall be discussing. So we start from the first proposal you have made on the subject of Members' scientific collaborators. With the proposed amendment from 15-11-1982 of Parliament's plenary decision, Members' scientific associates are also subject to the wage provisions of Chapter II of Law No.4354, by adjusting their basic salary to the new wages of civil servants. That is what the explanatory statement says and it is only an article, which refers to these issues. It concerns, as we have said, scientific associates and I can say that it is a small change in the better. Let's go to the rest. The preamble to the President's proposal to amend provisions of the Parliament's Rules of Procedure expressly states that "with the proposed amendment it is clarified for reasons of legal certainty that matters of transitional effect and application of individual provisions of the decision of the Greek Parliament of 28-6-2016". This is the last decision we made. The framework is foreseeable. In other words, it is this plenary decision and there are some clarifications, I would say, interventions on a number of issues. One is to extend the validity of Article 7. This has to do with the service councils, which must come out, which also requires elections to the members of the administrative boards of the elected representatives. Therefore, and because there is a time-consuming procedure to set up these, it is proposed to extend the validity of Article 7. It is an intervention to Rule 35, which we voted on. The second issue was debated in committee. It is a confirmation that we would say, even over-the-counter, of the order that we have voted on and of the co-workers and the forty posted officials of the House at Parliament's offices, particularly former Presidents of Parliament, former Prime Ministers and former Presidents of Parliament's groups. It is noted that it remains in force. We have said that this is surplus. This article shall apply. No one doubts that the article is valid. Therefore, it simply needed a repetition and confirmation of the issue for legal certainty. Finally, there was a phrase that should have come in concerning some officials of the Parliament and Article 43 of the full-scale decision, which is an amendment to Rule 152 of the former Rules of Procedure of the House as it stands. It is therefore proposed, because there was an inadvertent omission at that time, to add the phrase 'unfinished time' for the following reason: there was a risk that this provision, which had been voted on and passed by all, would become lex imperfecta, i.e. incomplete from one point to the next, because this phrase, which is decisive, is 'undetermined time'. These are the one, two and three articles of the President's proposals. Next, we refer to Article 4 which lays down the provisions of this amendment. These are, ladies and gentlemen, the proposed amendments from the President's side on the Rules of Procedure of the House and the others that we have pointed out, namely the 28-6-2016 plenary decision. As I said at the outset, the Committee on the Rules of Procedure, the Verification of Credentials and Immunities adopted almost unanimously. Therefore, we reiterate our position there, that we are positive about these proposals. I stop here because the debates on laws and regulations are of course very serious because they concern Parliament's Rules of Procedure, as well as issues relating to its functioning. However, many times tours within the laws and regulations are somewhat dull, for at least the rest. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u3	Thank you very much, Mr. Syrigos. Mrs Christodoulou, you have the floor on the unanimous proposal from the Committee on the Rules of Procedure, the Verification of Credentials and Immunities.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u4	Thank you, Mr President. Good morning, everyone and everyone. As you know, with the latest changes and amendments made to the law on 'whereabouts', it was established that the Audit Committee would have an internal rules of procedure. On 28 July, then, when we met at the first meeting of the committee, we drafted and adopted an internal regulation which, in accordance with the relevant Rule, stipulates that it should be adopted by Parliament. So we're presenting him to you today. I'll say a few words, 'cause there's no such thing as a great originality. The provisions shall be divided into three parts. The first part is the provisions that we replicate exactly the law and it has to do with its composition. So we put the new composition, which is now wide and defines who are made up of the alternates, what is its work and its mission. So we put these articles on. The second part of the provisions are provisions which we copy ad hoc the Code of Administrative Procedure, in particular the provisions relating to the operation, quorum and decisions, so that we can be in line with the general legal climate. The third part is provisions which we have decided on the basis of our experience as members of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection that they are necessary for the effectiveness of the committee. I think that most of these provisions are actually well known and some of them are necessary to enable the committee to function, without degenerating or becoming an area of competition. We have all agreed that absolute confidentiality and secrecy will be respected in all cases that are coming to committee. We also agreed that we would neither serve the immunity of political persons nor serve on a daily basis political purposes nor do we do a witch hunt on the basis of suggestions made either by the press or for other reasons. Therefore, I am at your disposal for any provisions which appear to you either original or some need explanations. I believe that we will work together, because this is a very important committee. In fact, there was a great deal of struggle in the negotiations, because creditors wanted in this law to include specific provisions on the functioning and composition of the committee. Thank you.

We, during the debate at the time of the bill, voted against this article because we disagreed with the composition of the committee. These concerns are multiplying today, with the Rules of Procedure, which we have in our hands, the proposal for a regulation on the operation of both this committee and the advisory committee, if the term is allowed. And I say that, because there are issues within which we have a lot of questions to ask, so that I do not use any other expression. As for example, when the committee is in a quorum, where at least two judges must be present. It may not be a party representative, but it must certainly be two judges. Secondly, the decision-making process is dangerous. In other words, it can be decided by three out of nine. Of course, with three out of six present, since the white vote - unprecedented - is seen as 'absent' and that in the successive votes it should be those who are voting against to coincide with one or the other majority. In other words, these are issues which raise a number of questions, Mr President, concerning these wordings that exist within. In our view, we must change them. Likewise, the issue of who can raise a matter on the agenda, which is forbidden on the substance. They must all be present, all nine, in order to raise a matter on the agenda or to ask for it by three of the nine before the meeting begins. So, basically, it turns into a ban. The last issue we want to raise, and in which we are clearly saying that we will not agree in practice, is the question of the other competences of the committee, of the assistant, which is responsible for the view of nominal coupons. You understand very well that the JRC will never enter this process of visas and nominations. In this respect, therefore, we are voting against both Regulations, Mr President, with these serious objections which we have. Thank you.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u10	Thank you, too. Mr. Tragakis has the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u11	I, Mr President, shall make two general remarks, since I say first of all that as far as the changes to the Rules of Procedure are concerned, as we voted about a month ago, we agree. This is not a substantial change. They're changes of clarification. And I think that one of them could be omitted from Article 2 because since it was the case that it was reintroduced and repeated, I do not know what it can offer. I will, however, stand by two points: my first point is that the Rules of Procedure of the House are a law of increased power. The Rules of Procedure of the House only change the Rules of Procedure. Since many times there are misunderstandings and there are proposals for changes in the Rules of Procedure, this cannot be done. The Rules of Procedure of Parliament only change from plenary and change only as a change in the Rules of Procedure of the House. One issue is that. The second concerns 'outside'. As far as 'outside' was concerned, there was a myth that the MPs were auditors and audited. That was a mistake. I have been the chairman of this committee for several years. The control was not carried out by the MPs. The audit was carried out by sworn auditors, as is now done. This is also provided for in the new law, whose regulation we are voting on today. So the same will be the case. There are some comments, which I shall make to Mrs Christodoulopoulos, concerning anonymous complaints. As we receive, almost every day, anonymous complaints about the "underground" policies, these anonymous complaints must be automatically filed. If there is a lasting complaint, as the Rules of Procedure do, the last complaint to be discussed, but to be discussed in plenary. And when I say 'Following', I believe that the provision that there is a quorum with the five members, but that two judges have to be present, is wrong for our committee. Because the committee has a different purpose. It is not intended to be a judicial review committee. I think that the House has been completely alienated from the whole process, because there is only one representative of a party and there is no representative of other parties. The committee could be more enlarged and they could also be representatives, if not everyone, because there are at least three or four parties, at least. However, I shall refer to something we discussed yesterday at the Conference of Presidents, Mr President, and I would like to make a reference to that. Yesterday at the Conference of Presidents, something has never been done before. We discussed the choice of the coordinator of the Channel of Parliament. So this is something that honors us all, because we had agreed on it during the debate on the Rules of Procedure, but it has been done. There was a person who I think was commonly accepted. He is a well-known journalist, who has also made a parliamentary editor, apart from other posts. However, some comments were made by colleagues, which should really be taken into account, which is why I mention them in the Minutes. First of all, as far as the Canal programme is concerned, I think the word that has been left yesterday from the whole procedure is the 'blue-way' relationship. In other words, there must be a two-way relationship now between the broadcasts of the Parliament's channel and the receivers, i.e. the viewers. That is why a number of proposals have been made, which I consider to be very positive. For example, there should be a daily report of the parliamentarians. Don't be more than five minutes, but be a day. Every day, there was a news bulletin on what is happening in Parliament - the same bill was passed, it went to the committee - so that the viewers could also be received. Secondly, the best relationship has been proposed, which can be created with the European Parliament. The European Parliament, I think, can offer too much and the exchange between the Channel of Parliament and the Channel of the European Parliament can help both channels very much. The third one - Mr Lykoudis, indeed, has made a very nice position - concerns the cultural events of the Parliament's channel. Because the channel of Parliament is showing cultural events, which may not have a large number of television viewings, but have a high quality of viewing and that is of great importance. Why do we not pay much attention to the'mechanisms', despite the fact that Parliament's sittings, as it turns out, Mr President, along with the public television, have arrived - please - have eight hundred thousand televisions - at large sittings, which we have here in Parliament, and which we are also giving to other private channels. The proposal that I made was as far as Parliament's record is concerned. The House has an amazing record. So we have to digitize this file, because there are excerpts and pre-wars, before 1940, which have to be gathered, as this will be a very important parliamentary archive for the place. That is what I wanted to say, Mr President. Of course, we are in favour of change. He had no substantial changes from what we had voted on last time. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u123	Thank you, Mr President. Mr Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, until now there has been a lack of a specific law on the award and execution of concessions, which has allowed a wide range of scope for ratification, which each time was based on different laws. At the same time, the exclusion of concessions from the scope of European directives created insecurity in terms of their legal status, which regulated the procedures for awarding and implementing procedures, and left a wide margin of discretion in the national laws of the Member States. These two problems, from the gap of law at national and European level, come to cover this bill. This new legislation shields the concession contracts legally, solves problems which have arisen in the past and eliminates the case-by-case specialisation of the concessionaire’s terms and procedures. Taking into account the contribution of concessions to the development of strategic infrastructure and services, and to the promotion of innovation, the proposed draft law is expected to be an important tool for the implementation of public investment. For the first time, therefore, the Greek legal order acquires a single legislative framework, which incorporates Union law, the acquis communautaire and at the same time ensures the maximum public interest. At the same time, this law is expected to serve as a catalyst for creating equal opportunities by supporting the national effort to return to economic growth and strengthen employment through the creation of new jobs. In summary, the main objectives of this legislative intervention are to introduce clear and effective arrangements, which ensure maximum transparency and equal treatment, prevent transaction routes, promote genuine and free competition, ensure the best cost and quality relationship and ensure the prevention and repression of corruption in the conclusion and execution of concessions. At the same time, these arrangements shall be provided to the contracting authorities and contracting entities the necessary other than those which are controlled flexibility to determine the conditions governing the award and performance of the concession, depending on the nature and scope of the contract, the way in which it is financed, but also the particular conditions for its implementation. This is achieved as, in addition to the provisions of Directive 2014/23/EU, the draft law incorporates and regulations of national law which introduce additional arrangements to those of the Directive or other matters that are deemed necessary to be safeguarded under the law, always in view of the achievement of its objectives. More specifically, in Part One of the bill, which includes Articles 1 to 28, issues relating to the purpose, scope and scope of the law are dealt with. The detailed description of the concept of the project contributes to the correct application of the rules and is confronted with distortions of the past resulting from the ambiguity of that concept. General principles include the concessionaire's risk-taking criterion, exempted contracts, simplified procurement rules and safeguarding confidentiality. It is clear that state activities, such as national defence, policing and justice, cannot be granted exclusive under the Constitution. Exemptions from concessions under this Law shall also include water supply and sewerage services as goods of general interest. Particular attention is paid to concessions between public sector operators. Such contracts shall not require a competitive procedure and shall be clearly and precisely defined on the basis of the judgments of the Court of Justice of Europe. By applying these conditions, healthy competition is not affected, but at the same time, public bodies are able to cooperate with each other in carrying out projects and services of social importance to the public benefit. In addition, it is clear to target the social mission of specific infrastructure and services, and for the first time social indicators are taken into account. Allows social cooperatives, social cooperatives with limited responsibility, social cooperatives with integration to implement policies that ensure social cohesion and strengthen and protect vulnerable social groups. It makes it compulsory to publish concessions using the National Electronic System of Public Contracts, which ensures speed and transparency. This contributes to the immediate information of interested parties, the publication of notices and the recording of a reliable database. At the same time, arrangements shall be made for the award of mixed contracts, the evaluation of which shall be judged on a case-by-case basis as regards their possible separation or not. If, for example, a contract includes elements which are a project, the current legal status is determined on the basis of the main subject-matter of the contract. In the event of doubt as to whether a contract concerns a project or service, which defines different technical terms and controls, the Technical Council for Public Works of the General Secretariat for Infrastructure of the Ministry of Infrastructure, Transport and Networks is responsible for deciding. In Part 2, from Articles 29 to 46, the rules for the award of concessions, such as the minimum time limits for receipt of tenders for participation, the drawing up of contract documents, the definition of technical specifications, the criteria for awarding the contract, the grounds for exclusion, the criteria for participation, the reasons for rejecting tenders and the manner in which tenders are to be completed and clarification. The regulation and the guarantees to combat corruption are important in this bill. Prevention measures are provided for in order to discourage infringements and conflict-related measures. In the event of unlawful and unfair behaviour, the contracting authority shall be given the possibility to exclude the economic operator from the award procedure. The technical and operational requirements contained in the contractual issues and the standardisation of declarations, the definition of the procedure, the quality assessment elements and the criteria for the ex ante determination are set out. The award procedure for a project concession may not be initiated without the prior completion of the necessary expropriations and the completion of the necessary archaeological investigations. There is a clear simplification of procedures and time-consuming bureaucratic procedures are being handled. It is worth mentioning, for example, that there is only one request for interest to be able to participate in the tender without requiring all necessary supporting documents. The supporting documents required shall be required to produce them after their selection only by the final contractor. In cases of possible ambiguity in the terms of the tender documents, it is also possible to correct for minor errors. At the same time, however, in order to combat corruption and with a reasoned report on damage to the public interest, it may be possible to cancel the award procedure, as well as to exclude from future contracts of economic operators who have been subject to offences for a period determined according to the seriousness of the offence and the timing of its termination. In Part Three, Articles 47 to 55 deal with questions relating to the financing of subcontracting, which supports small and medium-sized enterprises, the possibility of adapting a concession contract, without a new award procedure, where objectively unforeseen circumstances exist. In the fourth part of Articles 56 to 59, arrangements shall be made for the monitoring and submission of reports on the procedure for the registration of the elements of the procedures for the award and execution of concessions in the Central Register of Public Contracts. Provisions on the documentation of the award procedure of concessions shall also be introduced. Apart from describing the object of the project, there is now a clear illustration of the analytical budget for the work required, which was missing in the past and created problems. In the fifth part, Articles 60 to 66 shall include provisions on the legal protection of economic operators at the stage prior to the conclusion and on the resolution of disputes at the stage of its implementation. Finally, in Part Six, Articles 67 to 70, individual issues are regulated, such as the bodies carrying out contracts awarding and performance procedures, as well as transitional and repealing provisions. The objective of this draft law is to develop effective administrative interventions ensuring the provision of high quality projects and services to citizens. This is a bill that sets out uniform rules, which creates equal opportunities, first of all by laying down rules of transparency, healthy competition and, to a large extent, is facing a state-owned business environment that we have often seen in the past. The draft law establishes a legal security framework and ensures to the fullest extent the social and public interest. In conclusion and concisely, simplifying procedures, sound business action, social growth indicators, combating interoperable and corruption, transparency and efficiency are the legislative guarantees of this legislative initiative, which is coming to resolve the gap that has so far existed in the concession contracts. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u129	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the purpose of today's draft law is to harmonise Greek legislation with European Union Directive 2014/23 of 26 February 2014 on the award of concessions, as corrected by EL140/27-5-2016. Today's bill is divided into six parts. The first refers to the subject matter of the draft law, scope and definitions. The second part refers to the general rules for the award of concessions, the general principles governing the rules and the guarantee procedures, describing details of the publicity of notices, the electronic availability of tenders, the fight against corruption, the selection and the qualitative assessment of tenderers in tenders. The third part refers to the rules for the execution of concessions, i.e. the contractual framework and legislation, the preparation of contractual obligations, the financing of works, the possibilities of subcontracting, the possibility of modifying contracts, the solution and the award of contracts and the creation of special-purpose companies. The fourth part refers to the governance rules followed by the Single Independent Public Procurement Authority and the use of the Central Public Procurement Register, documentation of the award procedure and the performance of contracts. The fifth part refers to the legal protection of entities, when awarding contracts, the cooperation of the Greek authorities with the European Commission, the resolution of disputes in the performance of contracts, the extrajudicial and judicial resolution of issues arising. Part Six refers to the bodies for the award of concession contracts, which are repealed by the other transitional provisions and includes specific annexes. As usual, there is also a seventh part, which is the amendments to which I shall refer briefly. Let me note, ladies and gentlemen, that this bill was put into public consultation on 1 March 2016, within the reasonable deadlines, which it was necessary to vote on, within the deadlines set by the European Union. But, at the end of the day, he was tabled to the House a long time later and with great delay, and the House, the committee responsible, had to be forced to accept the urgency procedure while you could have avoided it. And today, we have the peculiar situation - and I mentioned this before - that urgent bills should be discussed in plenary, and the Committee on Production and Trade, while, at the same time, in the Senate, we are discussing an urgent bill, which was tabled last night, again the responsibility of the Committee on Production and Trade. However, we agreed with the urgency procedure because we thought there should be no further delay and an institutional gap extended. However, this does not relieve you of your responsibilities and, ladies and gentlemen, you must finally change speed. So far, you're moving on with a step of noticing. This practice cannot be perpetuated. You need to run, help the Greek economy return to growth, because we will eventually stay in the memorials and cuts. We voted 'NAH' in the first place. We believe that harmonisation with European law helps to deal with chronic pathogens. It is an effective tool in trying to acquire a modern and efficient public administration, attractive to both Greek and foreign investors and to small and medium-sized and large. At the same time, harmonisation with European law contributes to protecting the public interest and general interest. This government and privatizations have done and done three, if I'm not mistaken, so far works on the famous fast track. We, in the period 2014 at the Ministry of Development, placed particular emphasis on PPP projects. We have incorporated fifteen new projects, a total of 1.8 billion euros. We have re-activated allocations totalling EUR 5.5 billion. In total, twenty-eight PPP projects and concessions totalling nearly EUR 10 billion left a deposit to the next government. Many of these works today have frozen, planted, and are in danger of enduring. However, it is good that you are coming today to incorporate developments in European law into Greek law. Incorrectly, however, it was said by the Minister that in the past there was no legislation on PPPs. You forget, Minister, Law 3389/2005, a revolutionary law from which public-private partnerships began and, to your credit, you are currently maintaining basic provisions of Law 3389. It has been mentioned many times - and we discussed it yesterday, ladies and gentlemen - malfunctions in public procurement, failures in project executions. You said that, too, Mr Spirtzi, and you are right. It has been done many times in the past, no one is questioning it. For example, different materials were used than they needed, the measurements were not accurate, there was delays in the execution of the works, with the responsibility of anyone who finally came in and asked for the Greek public redefinition of his remuneration. These exactly themes are addressed by the institution of concessions and the PPP, where the maintenance, operation, execution, construction costs, the whole risk goes to the concessionaire, who in exchange for the operation in practice of the project, is paid for the investment he has made, either by the Greek State, either by the users of the project or by a combination of both. The concessionaire therefore assumes all responsibility. There was, therefore, appropriate legislation, and legislation is applied and it is good to apply it in the future, in order to reduce the risk of the Greek State from public works. And in this difficult economic environment, the PPP projects, using private capital, operate multiplierly in the Public Investment Program, which is systematically curtailed and now by the activation, which we all fear, the cutter, in the coming months, can be found again in the size of the Ministry of Finance, for which, unfortunately, ladies and gentlemen, the House will not be asked, because Parliament has now granted the right to budgetary policy to presiding decrees, to the Ministry of Finance and therefore to the institutions and to the quartet. It should be pointed out that it is not clear, Mr Ministers, whether the concessions of the Parliament will continue to be ratified, thereby guaranteeing the rights of the Greek State, but also creating legal certainty for the concessionaires resulting from the agreements. It is also necessary to provide for this possibility of granting contracts by law. We are also voting against Article 53 because provision should be made for the possibility of a direct contract between the contracting authority and the lenders to protect the interests of the latter in order to avoid a complaint. Two principles apply to concessions, which we accept. The first principle is that the infrastructural user alone pays for the construction and maintenance of the infrastructure. It does not pay the whole of the Greek people, it does not pay the whole of the citizens. He pays only the man who uses the infrastructure. The second principle of concessions is that, depending on the risk, the two partners take the risk of each being taken into account. In other words, the State, the State, takes the risk of speeding up expropriations, and the concessionaire takes the risk, if there are any reductions in vehicle flow, traffic load flows. In order to function, however, the concession system prerequisites something that is not yet apparent in our country. As you know, these conventions were previously excluded from the regulatory scope of the European Public Procurement Directives, whether they were completely fragmented and regionally regulated, if we were to put together a lack of an institutional framework next to it. So now the need pan-European was mature for a more modern piece of legislation dealing with these issues. In view of the particularities of this issue and of the differences between Member States, the directive avoids in detail the issues of concessions, establishing general principles and leaving details to the national authorities. ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u208	Thank you, Mr President. I would like to begin my position today with a point, in particular by addressing the good colleagues of the Staff Regulations. For days, the foreigner and the Greek press have been on the Georgiou case. Also, the other aim was to tarnish the alteration of data in the period 2004-2009 of Kostas Karamanlis, which country had the highest growth rates. I would therefore expect colleagues, especially the Officerial Response, to make even a comment on this case in the case of George. I would now like to say a word, because I tabled an amendment yesterday. With this I call on our fellow citizens, when they want to bring legal proceedings to deal with fines or taxes on taxes or other services, - because there is a 2010 law imposing cash payments equal to 50% of the value of the fine - to lodge safeguards, i.e. either to bear a property that is equal to 50% of the value, whether a guarantee letter or a guarantee letter, or a guarantor guaranteed by it for that amount of the debtor. I therefore call for this possibility to be given to our fellow citizens. And I think it is a facility that has no financial cost, while on the other hand it gives relief, it gives some light to all that blackness that has existed for years to our fellow citizens in our country. Ladies and gentlemen, so far neither nationally nor at European level existed a comprehensive legislative framework which regulates the procedure for awarding and implementing concessions. That is why we are now called upon to incorporate European Union Directive 23/2014 on public procurement and concession contracts, the application of the provisions of the directive, and other provisions of procedural law. The provisions of this draft law ensure the creation of a modern, transparent and effective legislative framework for the award and enforcement of concessions, which will guarantee compliance with the rules of secondary European law on public procurement, capable of ensuring the efficiency of procedures with a full guarantee of the public interest. The concession contracts are the most common form of public-private partnership. It is a balanced and flexible legal framework for the award of concessions - such as that sought by this directive - and it will ensure genuine and non-discriminatory market access for all economic operators, which will favour public investment in infrastructure and strategic services to citizens. Pursuant to Article 345 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, the Treaties in no way prejudice the rules of the Member States governing the status of ownership. Therefore, in accordance with the law of the European Union, including the concessions directive, public authorities remain completely free to carry out public tasks entrusted to them using their own resources or resources. However, if a public authority decides to award, for example, a private company to provide the service and conclude a concession contract with it, the provisions of the Directive must be complied with, in particular, the obligations relating to the selection and award criteria to be followed by entities awarding concessions. Given the rapid expansion of electronic purchasing systems, the Directives introduced into national law provide for specific rules for the implementation and operation of such systems, which ensure equal treatment of all economic operators who wish to participate in them. It is true, moreover, that the exclusion of concessions so far, from the scope of the European Public Procurement Directives, created insecurity regarding the legal status, which regulated their award procedures and left a wide margin of discretion in the national laws of the Member States. It shall, inter alia, lay down rules on procurement procedures by contracting authorities or contracting entities through concessions, partnerships between the public sector and in particular private companies, where they deal exclusively with the operation, maintenance and development of infrastructure, for example, ports, parking areas, roads with tolls or provide services of general economic interest, for example, energy, water distribution and waste disposal. Arrangements are also introduced to ensure rapid and effective legal protection and resolution of disputes, taking into account the specificities and complexity of the award procedures and the terms of the contractual framework governing their implementation. These Directives also specify how contracting authorities may contribute to the protection of the environment and to the promotion of sustainable development. Where the implementation of environmental management measures or schemes in the performance of the public contract is justified by the nature of the works or services, such measures or schemes may be required by contracting authorities. In order to ensure respect for European law, therefore, the committee has set up a system to verify whether European rules on public procurement are respected when carrying out projects or programmes financed by the Structural Funds and other European financial instruments, with a view to combating infringements of the rules, simplifying and modernising procedures, and opening up the markets in the European Union, as well as the effects of this opening up to third countries. In addition to the basic provisions, the characteristics of the new legal framework are the following areas, firstly, unification and homogeneity. The draft law regulates all works and services contracts awarded by contracting authorities and contracting entities in both the public sector and public utility services in a uniform and uniform manner. It also regulates all concessions regardless of their estimated value, i.e. also regulates concessions that fall below the threshold for application of Directive 2014/23/EU, as well as concessions for social and other services. It also aims at simplifying and speeding up competitive procedures, Article 41 of Directive 23. All the provisions of the draft law have been formulated with a view to simplifying and speeding up the maturity and the tendering of concessions. Indicatively, a mechanism for changing the burden of presentation of the supporting documentation of the qualitative selection criteria from the phase of participation in the final phase of award is introduced. In particular, the tenderers are required to provide only one responsible declaration - a questionnaire of quality selection to participate in the tender in which they confirm that they meet the quality selection criteria and only the final contractor is required to provide all the evidence to document the completion of those confirmed in the responsible declaration. This practice is expected to favour the participation of the largest number of economic operators in the procedures and thus to expand competition, speed up competition and substantially reduce bureaucratic burdens. It is also intended to implement social policy, Article 23 of the Directive. The draft law allows public bodies to grant exclusive rights to participate in the procedures for granting services to protected productive workshops, social cooperatives with limited liability, social cooperatives with integration, etc., in the context of the implementation of policies aimed at strengthening vulnerable groups. It introduces arrangements allowing public bodies to take into account environmental and social aspects throughout the award and performance cycle of the concession by means of appropriate technical specifications, qualitative selection and award criteria, as well as conditions for the performance of the contract. For the first time, it provides for exclusion of tenderers from award procedures or their infringement of provisions of environmental and labour legislation. It aims at enhanced publicity and information. The draft law defines the Central Public Procurement Register as the only mandatory point of publishing all stages of the concessions at national level - the publication of notices in other forms or electronic means of communication on a voluntary basis remains possible - which, on the one hand, will facilitate the information of stakeholders on business opportunities presented through concessions and, on the other, will contribute to the establishment of an electronic database for concessions and serve as the main means of exporting reliable statistics. In addition, in the direction of increasing transparency, free, unlimited and complete direct access is provided by electronic means to the tender documents in accordance with internationally established best practices. [[At this point, the Second Vice-President of Parliament, Mr GEORGIOS VARENTIS takes over]] Standardisation of declarations is expected to significantly simplify both the preparation of the procurement of concessions by public bodies and the participation of tenderers, as the procedures for the procedure will be clearly defined in the standard tender documents in advance, and the standardisation of the tender documents will ensure compliance with the existing legislation and will limit appeals against the terms of the tenders. In addition, it aims at effective legal protection at the pre-contractual stage and at the implementation stage, Article 60 of Directive 23. The draft law introduces arrangements under which rapid and effective legal protection is ensured at a stage prior to the conclusion of the contract and lays down flexible procedures for the resolution of disputes arising in the performance of concessions, taking into account the specificities and complexity of both the award procedures and the terms of the contractual framework governing their implementation. Such arrangements shall ensure that these disputes are resolved quickly and by extending the completion of the award and award procedures as a whole, thereby serving the public interest, but also by encouraging the participation of investors mainly from abroad. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] Mr President, I'm closing right now. Ladies and gentlemen, the concessions bill introduces public interest protection and corruption safeguards into the sensitive area of works and public procurement and is expected to be an important tool for the realisation of public investment that will serve growth, employment enhancement and the conversion of the economic climate. The Independent Greeks support it. Thank you.

He simply spoke of the suffocating deadlines that the Greek Government is facing, which, of course, was already known to Parliament since 17 August 2014, as was the final date for our country to be brought into line with European law. The end date, as you know, was April 17, 2016. So we're here three months late as a country now. The deadlines, which were not respected by Parliament, had arisen from our country's obligation to comply and integrate into the internal legal order of Directives 23/2014, 24/2014 and 25/2014 of the European Union, which Parliament is required to vote in favour of. He voted in favour yesterday and is invited to vote today on Directive 23/2014. In any event, this failure to plan the government, which leads it once again to legislate with the urgent procedures, could have been very simply avoided if the debate had begun even a week earlier. With better planning, there may have been various other disparities, such as the one made apparent to those who followed the process of hearing the bodies, where the Independent Authority for Public Contracts stated, by its representative, that it was informed of the changes to the bill in relation to the one you had put in mind, asking for their opinion and comments last April, from Parliament's website on the day that you posted the bill. The Independent Public Procurement Authority said that it was informed by Parliament's website on the day the bill was posted. Another practice of government legislating! In fact, as proof of his words, the representative of the Public Procurement Authority explained to us that I was amazed at the fact that he read in Article 375 of yesterday’s bill that the percentage of public contracts, which is the only source of revenue from the Authority, is reduced from 0,1 to 0,06. The surprise is quite justified, since in this case they will not have the money or their payroll. I would remind you that this principle has not yet been fully staffed and has not even been chaired and with the creation of its new arbitration body you have removed one of its most critical powers. We are not sure that the government has adequately considered the possibility of strengthening the Independent Public Procurement Authority so that it can take over this project, by making time and money, for the benefit of taxpayers, and after three years of operation it also has the relevant know-how and the necessary experienced personnel. I would like to point out that if the authority receives more than it needs, in order to cover well-known costs, then it returns 80% of the surplus to the Greek State, which made this decision even more curious for you to reduce its percentage to 0,06 on public procurement, but also to subtract it from its preliminary dimension. Finally, will we strengthen the independent authorities in this place? Will the Greek Parliament help in this? Do we want the independent authorities? Shall we, first of all, conclude what we want? Or will we create new bodies and agencies, new agencies, new bodies in addition and complementary to the work of the independent authorities, thereby risking creating more bureaucracy and promoting opacity? And because we are talking about public works contracts, we are actually talking about what the Greek people regard as the source of corruption and interlocutory in Greece. Any honesty of the government is vulnerable to criticism due to failures in the process of creating a new legislative framework ensuring the necessary sound procedural environment for the award and execution of public contracts. No one disputes that harmonisation with European directives serves the public interest and to some extent shields public procurement procedures from well-known old practices that favour corruption and complexity. The same applies to efforts to bring together, update and codify the relevant laws, so that a brake can enter into the relevant diversity and bureaucracy and strengthen the concept of good administration. Of course, it is also very good that a national objective is being made for the proper use of technology in order to ensure conditions of transparency and avoid infringibility and impunity. Indeed, the incorporation of Directive 23 brings about substantial changes. But can we talk about a new era in the awarding and execution of public contracts? I think not. This is because, while we recognize the government's good intentions, we do not see them formulated in a clear way in the articles of these bills. On the contrary, there are ambiguities. Of course, however, this is a great step forward for our country, which is being delayed and costed for the Greek taxpayer. It is important, for example, that subcontracting is recognised for the first time, that works fragmentation is possible not as a means of waste and corruption, but as a means of meeting current needs. In the positive measures, too, we measure the fact that effective and multi-level operation, under Article 38 of yesterday's bill, of the Central Electronics Register of Public Contracts, is being strengthened in this famous register. In fact, the Government is trying, with this draft law, to correct misappropriate texts and to strengthen its efforts at European level to codify procedures for the award and execution of public contracts and to limit bureaucracy, in the light of internationally recognised good practice and with a view to simplifying procedures, ensuring the principle of proportionality, ensuring transparency and quality in all manifestations and extensions of the conclusion, supervision and execution of public works and concessions. We must support this effort, which aims to enhance transparency and stimulate healthy entrepreneurship through cooperation between the public and private sectors. Although PPP issues have in the past been a field of strong controversy, the Greek Government is now sincerely reintroducing an objective view of things and choosing to follow the European path of establishing common rules, which will reduce the distortions resulting from national legislation, in a common open market such as the European market. At the same time, the adoption of these directives and of the other European national parliaments also reveals the precise size of State intervention, which the European economic thought accepts, regarding the functioning of the common market. The demand for transparency is universal and the introduction of rules for its security is an urgent need for democracy and a strong social demand. The European institutions and the individual states must, in order to confirm their high cultural level, exemplaryly institutionalise good and effective practices of good public money management through the transparent conclusion and execution of public contracts, which in Greece account for almost half of the size of public sector finances, while in Europe public procurement reaches up to 18% of GDP. The Centro Association will continue to support consistently and decisively the country's European path. We will vote in favour of this bill at first, we will reserve some articles and for the amendments our parliamentary representative, our dear Parliamentary representative, will speak. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u218	Mrs. Thalerite, you have the floor. Then Mrs Chrysosvelloni will take the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u219	Ladies and gentlemen, Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, this draft law for the first time in Greece creates a comprehensive and comprehensive legislative framework which regulates the procedure for the award and execution of concessions. The awarding of concessions is a matter of particular importance, particularly in the current context of our country's serious budgetary constraints and economic difficulties. Legislation on concessions is considered one of the measures which can help to ensure a more efficient distribution of public money, creating the conditions for a competitive and transparent award of a contract. Ladies and gentlemen, as you know, as you are aware, the tendering and implementation of concession contracts has been based on the provision of Article 11 of the Public Works Code and on the provisions of the institutional content for the implementation of public-private partnership projects under Law 3389/2005, which, however, only apply to specific projects which form part of this scheme. The unclear and inadequate institutional framework has led to the practice of the concession agreements being ratified by Parliament by law, primarily in order to satisfy the needs of safeguarding trade relations, but also in order to introduce derogations from existing legislation, so as to facilitate and accelerate the implementation of projects which were facing serious delays. This procedure has had a number of malfunctions, as we all know, since it caused problems of either legal nature, for example, the amendment of the conventions required ratification by Parliament and of practical importance, such as major delays in the completion of the work procedures. Furthermore, the current situation of the lack of legal certainty and of ever-changing legislation caused serious economic deficiencies and was detrimental to both contracting authorities and economic operators, as well as to users of such services. Enterprises and banks were constantly at risk, committing large sums of money to long-term concessions. The public authorities could be faced with persistent complaints and a solution to their contracts. Against this background, therefore, this draft law comes in order to highlight and regulate the specific features of the contracts, stressing the different way in which the projects are awarded and executed by public contracts, such as reduced bureaucratic procedures, funding and transfer to the concessionaire of the financial risk of the holding. In other words, it is created in a long-term, secure and reliable legislative framework, which on the one hand ensures healthy competition with a genuine and non-discriminatory market access for economic operators and on the other hand, the state can now ensure quality services for citizens at better prices and develop a more long-term strategy for infrastructure and services - which is still missing. The purpose of this legislative initiative, beyond the incorporation of Directive 2014/23/EU, is therefore that it is a national planning where a large part of the arrangements are national regulations. These arrangements reflect the SYRIZA's policy and intention for balanced and sustainable development. What are the innovative elements of this draft law? His social character. What do we mean by that? For the first time the financial model of the arrangement includes social indicators and criteria, based on the economic conditions in force at the time of the contract, for economic development and for the protection of vulnerable and vulnerable social groups. What else does this bill give? It enables public bodies to grant exclusive rights to participate in the award procedures of service concessions to protected productive workshops, limited social cooperatives and social cooperatives. Indeed, it creates a framework for social cohesion policies that strengthen vulnerable groups. What's an extra import? It introduces arrangements allowing public bodies to take into account environmental and social aspects throughout the award and performance cycle through appropriate technical specifications, qualitative selection and award criteria, as well as conditions for the performance of the contract. For the first time, it is foreseen that the exclusion of tenderers from a tender procedure or their infringement of environmental and labour law provisions is justified. It is not possible, ladies and gentlemen, to keep in mind that the permanent residents of a municipality or the unemployed of a region or those with a disability are not taken into account in such large infrastructure projects, or that there is no provision for the time for the project to be granted at such times that the economically weak strata can use these structures, which have arisen from the concession. It is clear that these measures meet the principles of the social justice of the SYRIZA, ensuring social cohesion and solidarity. I would also like to focus on another innovative element in the fight against corruption and opacity. We all know very well that the existing system of public contracts and concessions is a system that has rightly been criticised as opaque, that it produces corruption and complexity and leads to a systematic breach of public interest. In fact, we all remember in the recent past that it has led to numerous contracts at a disproportionate cost to the Greek people. Also, this bill comes precisely to regulate these guarantees of transparency, combating fraud, conflict of interest, cosmopolitanism and corruption. It also regulates the public interest very well. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] I will say here - and I shall end, Mr President - that there is a clear blockade of tenderers, since they are found to be using illegal practices or unfair means to distort competition or affect the outcome of the award procedure. Publicity and information are also being strengthened through the electronic register of public contracts, which, on the one hand, is a tool for the publication of concessions, facilitates entrepreneurs to have better opportunities and, on the other, helps to obtain an electronic database on concession contracts and, therefore, will be a key tool for making use of them as an export of reliable statistics. Finally, I would like to focus on the latest innovatory element of my speech in this bill, which concerns achieving the best relationship between costs and quality, namely, dealing with investment delays due to expropriation of environmental authorisations and archaeological research. All those who are involved and on this subject have understood that contracts and projects which could not be implemented were approved, precisely because all the above authorisations for environmental approval and archaeological research were pending. We have wasted as a country millions of euros for these investments. So this bill does not approve contracts, which cannot be started unless the necessary expropriations have been made and have not been...

ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u269	Mr President, Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, today we are discussing a draft law which refers to a kind of public procurement that has been set up to the Greek people in a bad manner. They were identified as tolls on the national roads and were regarded as privileged for a small group of contractors, whose practice differs slightly from that of cartels. Unfortunately, to date, the lack of relevant legislation, particularly in our country, along with the exclusion of concessions from the scope of the European directives, has enabled the various concessions to be ratified by reference to different laws each time. Ladies and gentlemen, was there an institutional framework for concessions? Oh, no. Why did each project come out of a separate bill to ensure that it works and above all to secure the contractors? I think that examples such as the unacceptable 2007 contracts over the Suffle era, as well as the unacceptable 2013 revisions, during the Chrysochide era, supported only business interests. If you look at the recitals of these laws, you will see that they played an important role in reviewing contracts because they lost, that is, they did not benefit the concessionaires. I will give you some examples of what contracts these were. Do you know that in 2007 the contracts predicted that expropriations would be made in four months? Even today in the E65 there are no primary decisions. Do you know that they predicted that the archaeological studies would be carried out in five, six months? They're not finished yet. Of course, we need to keep something else: the only thing that has been observed in these agreements, apart from what has served the concessionaires, has been tolls, for which the situation of the Greek people has in no way been taken into account. I'm going to tell you something else: This high toll procedure what it created? It has created a movement from the secondary national road network, with what this means - and for its maintenance and all - but also from the side road network, from which crossing is prohibited because it must serve the side road traffic. This resulted in the insecurity of the legal regime that regulates the award procedures and, as always, when similar cases are treated with different legal bases and, where appropriate, the specificity of the terms and procedures, left room for the actions and behaviour involved that gave the people the unfavourable views that I have mentioned. It must not, however, escape our attention that concessions are the right instrument, through which public bodies can make use of the experience and know-how of the private sector for the purpose of efficient provision to citizens of high quality services and works. Taking into account the contribution made by concessions to the development of strategic infrastructure and services, but also to the promotion of innovation, it is certain that we cannot and should not demolish them from the reality of the country's public works. The proposed draft law harmonises Greek legislation with Directive 23/2014 and national rules are adopted in implementing directives. The aim is to create a single and full legislative framework for regulating the award and performance of concessions. This legislative framework, I believe, will be an important tool for realising public investment and turning the negative economic climate. It will also contribute crucially to the creation of business opportunities. It will support the country's effort to return to economic growth and strengthen employment through the creation of new jobs. Finally, public administration will have a new, uniform, modern and many innovative legal instrument to safeguard the public interest. Acknowledging the specificities of this category of contracts, both in terms of awarding and implementing, and in particular in terms of their financing, the arrangements introduced today I consider that they ensure rapid, effective and legitimate protection and resolution of disputes in the field of concessions. They clearly guarantee transparency and equal treatment. They prevent suspicious transactions. They promote genuine, healthy and free competition. They shall ensure the best cost and quality relationship and shall guarantee the prevention and repression of corruption in the conclusion and execution of such contracts. It is worth noting that mixed contracts have been dealt with, including projects in the classic concept of the term. It is foreseen that the current legal regime is determined on the basis of the main subject-matter of the contract and for any doubt as to it that the Technical Council for Public Works of the General Secretariat for Infrastructure of the Ministry of Infrastructure, Transport and Networks is appointed. For the first time, the detailed budget of the concession project is included in the contractual issues to deal with problems in illustrating the situation, the development of a project. For the first time also, the financial model includes social indicators and criteria based on the economic conditions in our country at the time of their formation, for economic development and for the protection of vulnerable and vulnerable social groups. Finally, there is a clear and precise definition of the conditions that ensure healthy economic competition in the event of concessions between public bodies, taking into account the case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union. We therefore have in front of us, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, a bill aimed at and I believe that it achieves the establishment of a separate, uniform and uniform regulatory framework for concessions, in accordance with international legislative standards and best practices, in order to address the distortions of the past. Just yesterday we voted for a huge piece of legislation on public procurement for studies, works, supplies and services, except, of course, for concessions. What activities will be chosen to be carried out in one way or another is a privilege, but also a responsibility of the public authority, which must be exercised in the light of the public interest. However, a common component must be the targeting of public money for actions and projects which are a direct need for the whole of society. It is time and because of the economic crisis, which, if anything, has made us wiser, to leave aside the pharaonic-type and sized projects that have dominated our thinking and practice over the past decade. Not projects for projects, but for the social whole, for the balanced development of every corner of our country. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u272	And we thank you for the time economy. Mr. Ioannis Sarakiotis from SYRIZA has the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u273	Mr Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, following the vote in plenary yesterday on the draft law of the Ministry of Economy, Development and Tourism on public works, supplies and services contracts, the other parallel, related and many supplementary bills, especially for the award and execution of concessions. As developed in the preparation of the draft law in the relevant legislators, it aims to harmonise the Greek legislation with Directive 2014/23 and to adopt national regulations, implementing the Directive, in order to create a single and comprehensive legislative framework for regulating the award and performance of concessions. First of all, as the rapporteur and the Ministers responsible have pointed out, there has so far been neither an institutional framework for concessions nor for public-private partnerships. It was a continuation of public works, which is why in most cases it came to the House to be ratified by law. They were not exactly public works and studies. It wasn't provisions. It was something in between. Therefore, this legislative initiative by the Government to adopt a coherent law, with uniform specific rules for the award and performance of concession contracts, specific content in contractual issues, financing issues, contracts which are always given in concessions. To date, the exclusion of concessions from the scope of the European Directives created insecurity regarding the legal regime governing its award procedures and left a great deal of margin for assessment in the national legislation of the Member States. Especially in our country, the lack of specific legislation has created a wide-ranging distorting field for ratification of concessions, each time based on different laws. This new legislation aims at the legal immunisation of these contracts, the resolution of problems encountered in the past, the collapse of the situation where the licensee specialises in the selection of terms and procedures. At the same time, arrangements are introduced for a substantive and procedural framework which ensures the rapid and effective protection and resolution of disputes in the field of concessions, taking into account the specificities and complexity of both the award procedures and the terms of the contractual framework governing their implementation. This ensures the pursuit of the main objectives of legislative intervention, i.e. the introduction of clear and effective framework regulations, ensuring maximum transparency and equal treatment, preventing transaction routes, promoting genuine and free competition, ensuring the best cost and quality relationship and ensuring the prevention and repression of corruption in the conclusion and execution of these contracts. In addition to the provisions of this directive, the draft law also incorporates national legislation, which introduce regulations complementary to those of the directive, or other matters which were deemed necessary to be safeguarded under the law, in view of the achievement of its objectives. Finally, when drawing up the draft law, there was special care to limit the number of delegated acts adopted, so that the adoption of each of them would not be a precondition for the application of the law. I also have to stand by the main innovations introduced by the current draft law, which is quite clear what is their aim and what they intend to heal. The detailed definition of the concept of the project is thus made clear in such a way that there is no ambiguity about what is precisely a project concession, which in the past has created many distortions about the correct application of the applicable rules. It shall avoid infringement or circumvention of the law in the event of mixed contracts including works. Where a contract includes elements which constitute a project within the meaning of this Article, the current legal regime shall be determined on the basis of the main subject-matter of the contract. In the event of doubt as to whether a contract concerns a project or service, which defines different technical terms and controls, it is the Public Works Council of the Ministry of Infrastructure's General Secretariat for Infrastructure. Third, the procedure for awarding a project concession cannot be initiated without the prior completion of the necessary expropriations and completion of the necessary investigations or archaeological work. Let us not forget how much billions have been allocated since Mr Soufflias began the famous concessions to date. Fourthly, for the first time, the detailed budget for the concession project is included in the contractual issues, in order to address problems in illustrating the situation in the development of a project. Apart from describing the object of the project, the detailed budget of the work required is now clear. In addition, for the first time the financial model includes social indicators and criteria, based on the economic conditions in force in the country at the time of its formation, for economic development and for the protection of vulnerable and vulnerable social groups. There is no provision for people with disabilities and there is no provision for permanent residents - even at the level of the Callicratic municipality - if there is no provision for the unemployed. Now that's what we're talking about. It was no longer tolerated that other ways, other sizes, variables, which can be used by the public - such as the time of the concession - could also be economically impossible to use these infrastructures in times of crisis. Turns of this type now they're pulling out. Taking into account the contribution of concessions to the development of strategic infrastructure and services and to the promotion of innovation, the proposed draft law will be an important tool for the implementation of public investments and for the conversion of the negative economic climate. The Greek legal order now acquires a single legislative framework incorporating Union law, the acquis communautaire and at the same time ensures the maximum public interest. For all these reasons, I too welcome this legislative initiative by the Government. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u276	Thank you, too. Mr. Chogas has the floor from SYRIZA.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u277	Thank you, Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I think that the answer to a previous speech by the Representative of the Centro Association is due. In my opinion, the criticisms made, unnecessarily and unrelentingly, have been very acute. ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u278	Documented, Mr. Comrade.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-08-03-S1-commons.u279	We'll see if they're documented. You can answer it if you want. All of this in the sense of the concept of urgency and of the way in which this legislation is introduced, but also what is going on. This is an entirely unfounded position because it does not justify the reasons which the Minister has claimed if they are valid or unfounded. The concept of urgency is obviously legal, since it exists in law. So if the speaker of the Centroon wants to turn against the President of Parliament and the legislature who already has the order. The provision is existing and legal. It should therefore explain why the urgency procedure is unfounded. That would be real if the reasons put forward by the relevant Minister were unfounded. Against the words of the relevant Minister in no way has it turned. He has made a populist policy, with a sterile opposition structure and obviously is involved in a counterproductive parliamentary process. I think she was totally wrong, with a totally populist conception. As regards the draft law, this is a draft law concerning the award and execution of concessions and other services and adapting to Directive 2014/23 of the European Union. The draft law introduces for the first time a uniform and complete legislative framework for the award and enforcement of concessions for works and services. The promoted legislative initiative incorporates Directive 23 on the award of concessions and at the same time adopts national rules. The concession contracts are widespread forms of partnership between the public sector and private undertakings, which have proved to be extremely useful, especially in the implementation of infrastructure, where traditionally concession contracts are a permanent practice in building, technical works, roads, motorways, etc. Although concessions are used in areas of vital importance to the economy and growth, to date there has been no comprehensive legislative framework which regulates the award and execution process and the relevant provisions have been incomplete and dispersed. Increased investment needs combined with the need to save public resources make concessions a valuable tool for the growth of the economy and increase employment through private capital. In view of the specific nature of concession contracts and their differences in public procurement, in particular because of their financing, the new arrangements lay down the basic rules and the framework and provide the contracting authorities and bodies with the necessary, other than audited, flexibility to determine the individual conditions. In accordance with the provisions of this draft law, in the process of awarding concessions, contracting authorities shall treat economic operators equally and impartially and shall act in a transparent and proportionate manner. It shall be explicitly prohibited to act in a discriminatory manner and to provide information that may favour certain candidates at the expense of others. In the same context, they shall disclose the intention to award the concession by publishing a notice containing clear and complete conditions for the award and execution of all interested parties to be informed. They shall ensure that a sufficient number of candidates are involved in order to ensure effective competition and shall communicate the results of the procedure to all interested parties. In order to meet the requirements of publicity and transparency, the Central Electronic Contract Register shall be defined as a mandatory point of publication at national level of all stages of concessions, irrespective of their estimated value. The notices, contracts and amendments thereto shall be published and the contracting authorities shall provide the entity concerned with unlimited and complete electronic access to all general conditions for the performance of the award procedure. At the same time, the new legal framework is established with a view to simplifying and speeding up the procedures for awarding concessions. In particular, it is foreseen that contracting authorities may at first instance accept the submission of a qualitative selection questionnaire by the candidate economic operators, which includes a declaration that they fulfil the selection criteria and that they are not satisfied with the reasons for exclusion. Only if the tenderer is selected and before the conclusion of the contract is notified to submit all the supporting documents required to prove the statements and data submitted by the qualitative selection questionnaire. This widened competition and increased the participation of economic operators, while at the same time speeding up competition free from procedural and bureaucratic burdens. The concession contracts shall be awarded on the basis of objective criteria, taking into account the financial adequacy of economic operators, potential for growth and innovation in the public interest. As a result, in the process of selecting and evaluating candidates, the economic operator who has been convicted of illegal acts such as corruption, fraud, involvement in criminal organisation, money laundering, child labour and other forms of trafficking in human beings are excluded. In addition, it is excluded from the participation of the economic operator which has an express obligation to comply with the rules of environmental, social and labour law, or acting unfairly, with a view to distorting competition. This exclusion system seeks to shield the public interest and to select reliable contractors. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] Just a moment, please, Mr President. In the context of social policy, and with a view to strengthening vulnerable and vulnerable groups, their social inclusion and ensuring equal opportunities for all, the new draft law enables contracting authorities to grant exclusive right to participate in procurement procedures for protected workshops, social cooperatives and social cooperatives to integrate in the context of protected employment programmes for persons with disabilities or disabled persons in the labour market. Ladies and gentlemen, the new draft law on concessions establishes a stable legal environment and ends the lack of legal certainty, which is due to the absence of clear provisions. The consolidation and homogeneity of the new rules create ground for investment in high-quality public services and infrastructure, strengthening the competitiveness, economic development and productive reconstruction of the country, which oblig us in the unconditional adoption of this draft law and the acceptance of its provisions. Thank you.

Today we incorporate Directive 23/2014 into national legislation on the award and enforcement of concessions and adopt national regulations and implementing the directive and our own options in order to create a single and comprehensive legislative framework for regulating the award and enforcement of concessions. The exemption of concessions to date from the scope of the European Public Procurement Directives created insecurity in relation to the legal regime governing their award procedures and left a wide margin of discretion in the national laws of the Member States or in the light of the issues of tenders which were made every time in any different work. The aim of the draft law is therefore to establish an autonomous, uniform and uniform regulatory framework, in accordance with international legislative standards and accepted best practices, as well as the principles and rules of primary and secondary Union law, which regulate the sector of concessions. In addition, I would point out that, in addition to the arrangements of Directive 2014/23/EU, the draft law incorporates Union law regulation which introduce supplementary regulation of the directive and issues that are deemed necessary and should be implemented, in view of the achievement of its objectives. [[At this point, the closing bell of the speaking time of the Minister is ringing.]] Madam President, I would like three minutes, your tolerance. I'll mention the innovations of the bill. The first innovation is that, at last, the concept of the project is defined, which has created many ambiguities and distortions. The third - which is very critical, I will explain it in detail - is the award procedure for a project contract.
